Do NOT re-pick: Jan Davidsz. de Heem (#557), Balthasar van der Ast (#556), Roelant Savery (#555), Gillis van Coninxloo (#554), Hendrick Goudt (#553), Adam Elsheimer (#552), Aert van der Neer (#551), Eglon Hendrik van der Neer (#550), Adriaen van der Werff (#549), Godfried Schalcken (#548), Aert de Gelder (#547), Arnold Houbraken (#546), Jacobus Houbraken (#545), Bernard Picart (#544), Reinier Vinkeles (#543), Jan Punt (#542), Jacob de Wit (#541), Wilhelm von Kobell (#540), Dirck van Bergen (#539), Adriaen van de Velde (#538), Evert Oudendijck (#537), Cornelis Dusart (#536), Adriaen van Ostade (#535), Andries Both (#534), Jan Both (#533), Hendrick Bloemaert (#532), Abraham Bloemaert (#531), Dirck van Baburen (#530), Hendrick ter Brugghen (#529), Gerard van Honthorst (#528), Michiel Jansz van Mierevelt (#527), Frans Pourbus the Younger (#526), Marcus Gheeraerts the Younger (#525), Isaac Oliver (#524), Nicholas Hilliard (#523), Steven van Herwijck (#522), Jacques Jonghelinck (#521), Jacopo da Trezzo (#520), Pompeo Leoni (#519), Francesco Melzi (#518), Leonardo da Vinci (#517), Michelangelo Buonarroti (#516), Sebastiano del Piombo (#515), Titian (#514), Paolo Veronese (#513), Jacopo Ligozzi (#512), Bartolomeo Bimbi (#511), Lorenzo Fratellini (#510), Giovanna Fratellini (#509), Rosalba Carriera (#508), Maurice-Quentin de La Tour (#507), Joseph Ducreux (#506), Francois Dumont (#505), or anyone already studied.)

## Corrections

Leg 1 corrections (C709-C726), as reported:

- C709. "Inherited the workshop" is undocumented -- the central flag of this leg. No source read this leg or cited in #557 says Cornelis inherited or took over a workshop. The phrase was coined in #557 leg 3 (L306: "the son who inherited the workshop") and carried into #559 L288. Against it: the father's Utrecht workshop went to Mignon in 1672 (#557 leg 1 F34); Cornelis worked as a self-employed painter in the north from 1667 (Ketterer P7 L16). D2 and D6 fail on this element. The #560 study must not repeat the phrase as fact; the defensible form is "trained in his father's Antwerp studio, Guild member 1660, then self-employed in Utrecht, IJsselstein and The Hague".
- C710. D7 misattributes the four mention-lines. The paragraph says "#556 entry's Future-candidates paragraph and its Depth line"; the #556 Depth line (log L5169) never mentions Cornelis. The four lines are #556 FC (5172), #557 Depth (5177), #557 FC (5180), #558 FC (5188).
- C711. "Why Seghers over Cornelis de Heem..." is quoted but does not exist. Grep across the log, the studies dir and the #557 leg files finds the phrase only in clara-peeters.md L288 itself. Log L5180 (the #557 FC paragraph) contains no such phrase.
- C712. "Sotheby's read end to end" in #557 is unsupported. #557 leg 3's pages table lists only four Wikipedia pages read end to end; Sotheby's appears in #557 only as search citations (leg 3 S4). Leg 1's and leg 2's pages tables also list no Sotheby's page. The 46-line count belongs to Wikipedia alone.
- C713. "Jewel-like" is an unsourced gloss. It appears in no source read; it comes from #557 leg 3 L307. D3 fails in the letter on this one word.
- C714. Van der Ast as "the teacher" is overstated. Sources say "probably" / "may have been his teacher" (#557 leg 1 D6/F5; #556 study file L224). No document proves the pupillage. D4 fails in the letter on the definite article.
- C715. David Corneliszoon de Heem death date conflicts. Wikipedia Cornelis article L10: "(1663 - after 1718)"; the same article's family tree L18-19: "David Cornelisz de Heem 1663-1701"; invaluable (skimmed) also gives 1663-1701. #559 already flags this as "1663 - after 1718 / 1701?". Carry the flag; do not pick a side without a better source.
- C716. Invaluable.com gives an impossible guild year. Its artist page text (search excerpt, skimmed) says Cornelis "in 1630 ... was accepted as a master by the St. Luke's Guild" -- impossible: he was born in 1631. The correct year is 1660 (Sotheby's, Ketterer, Wikipedia). Do not use that page's 1630.
- C717. Auto-generated artwork-facts pages date a work after his death. Topimpressionists (and the same text on wahooart) date "Fruit still life with oysters" to 1699 -- four years after he died in 1695. Those pages are unreliable for dates; do not cite them.
- C718. The log's doorway tally does not add up. #559's entry (the 180th check) records "one hundred and thirty-two holds and fifty-four non-holds" -- 132 + 54 = 186, six more than the 180 checks run. The parent brief faithfully repeats the log's numbers; the six-count excess is pre-existing in the log, not introduced by this leg. After this check (non-hold), the log's method would give 132 holds / 55 non-holds against 181 checks. The parent may want to reconcile the tally.
- C719. "All carries" is imprecise. The L5177 mention ("Cornelis de Heem's cleared exclusion (see C661)") sits in the #557 Depth line and is a correction note, not a Future-candidates carry. Substance is unaffected (no mention is a study), but the wording overstates.
- C720. The 1691/92 return: from where? Ketterer P7 L23: "In 1691/92 Cornelis de Heem returned from IJsselstein to Antwerp". Invaluable (skimmed) says he taught his son David in The Hague and returned with him in 1691/92. Ketterer has him in The Hague until at least 1684, so "from IJsselstein" is loose. Minor; the return year 1691/92 is agreed.
- C721. Wikipedia's family tree uses the old death year for the father. Tree L18: "Jan Davidsz. de Heem 1606-1683"; Meijer (2016, archival) gives a probable burial 10 February 1684 (#557 leg 1 F29), and the #557 study uses "before 26 April 1684". The tree's 1683 is the superseded form; Sotheby's lot note likewise writes the father as "1606-1683/4" (P2 L47).
- C722. The "jansz" parenthetical is true only as a filename search. grep -ril "jansz" over ~/workspace/muse-soul/studies/ returns 35 files (including jan-davidsz-de-heem.md, which discusses Jan Jansz. at length). The only *jansz* FILENAME is michiel-jansz-van-mierevelt.md. Not a D7 driver (the task's D7 does not include it); recorded so the parent does not repeat the parenthetical as a content-grep claim.
- C723. Mauritshuis Fruit Still Life date/size unverified. Niceartgallery (skimmed) says 1695, 65 x 50 cm. The Mauritshuis object page was not read this leg (no verbatim URL obtained). Do not cite 1695 / 65 x 50 as verified.
- C724. Verendael's death day-month unverified. #559 gives "11 August 1691"; Wikipedia Verendael P4 L4 gives only "1640 in Antwerp - 1691 in Antwerp" and L10 gives the baptism (19 February 1640). The day-month needs a source check; not verified this leg.
- C725. Jan Jansz. de Heem death date conflicts. Wikipedia Cornelis article's family tree L18-19 gives "Jan Janszoon de Heem 1650-1695"; #559 gives "bapt. 2 July 1650 - after 1695". Carry both; do not pick a side without a better source.
- C726. Rijksmuseum Vanitas Still-Life with Musical Instruments: attribution contested in secondary sources. Artsdot/wahooart/allpaintingsstore attribute the 153 x 166 cm Rijksmuseum vanitas to Cornelis (c. 1661-1668); visual-arts-cork lists the same-titled c. 1660s Rijksmuseum picture among JAN Davidsz. de Heem's works. Neither is a museum source and the Rijksmuseum page was not read this leg. Do not cite this picture as Cornelis's without verifying the Rijksmuseum object record.

Leg 3 corrections (C740-C748), as reported:

- C740. Correction to the parent's framing: the example "#558 block 17 -> 16" is wrong. The #558 block (daniel-seghers.md line 285) has 18 names, not 17 -- Adriaen van Utrecht was missed in the count. Correct adjustments: #559 block 17 -> 16; #558 block 18 -> 17; #557 block 20 -> 19. Also: there are NO separate carried #558 or #557 block paragraphs in the #559 file; per C700/C705 the #559 file's "New from #559" block of 17 IS the #558 file's "New from #558" block of 18 minus Peeters (now the subject).
- C741. Flag on C700/C705 (via #558 C669): "adjusted the #557 block 18 to 17" does not match the file count. The #557 block in jan-davidsz-de-heem.md line 313 has 20 names; 19 were carried into #558 (13 in the new #558 block + 6 still-carried) and 1 dropped (David Corneliszoon, excluded). The "18" appears to count the #558 new block, not the #557 block. C700/C705's "block of 18" for #558 itself is correct and verified this leg.
- C742. #559 D2/D6 "Guild master in 1660" overstates the source: Wikipedia says Cornelis "became a member of the Antwerp painters' guild in 1660"; Ketterer says "accepted as independent painter". No source read calls him master in 1660.
- C743. #559 D2/D6 "inherited the workshop" is not sourced: no source read supports "inherited". The phrase is #557's own gloss (jan-davidsz-de-heem.md line 313), repeated verbatim into daniel-seghers.md line 285 and clara-peeters.md lines 288/290. A chain invention, not a sourced fact.
- C744. #559 D4 "the teacher in the Utrecht studio" (van der Ast) is flat where every source hedges: #557 file L5 ("Meijer can find no document for it"), #557 C9 ("probably", "may have been", "probable teacher", Thyssen "Nothing is known of De Heem's training"). Getty ULAN lists van der Ast as de Heem's teacher, but the flat form is not supported.
- C745. Cornelis Kick's birth year: Wikipedia bapt. 12 March 1634; V and A "(1631/34-1681)"; NGA "(1635-1681)". Use bapt. 12 March 1634 as the anchored date.
- C746. Jean-Baptiste Monnoyer: Dictionary of National Biography gives birth 19 July 1634 vs Wikipedia 12 January 1636; the de Heem pupillage rests on Sotheby's alone and is squeezed by "in Paris by 1650".
- C747. Jan Baptist Morel's Brussels Guild date: Wikipedia 1699 vs lot-art 1669 (1669 is impossible at age 7; 1699 is the anchored date).
- C748. Spelling split: "David Corneliszoon de Heem" vs "David Cornelisz de Heem" both circulate; the log uses "Corneliszoon" (3 occurrences / 3 lines), "Cornelisz" has 0 log occurrences. The study should pick one spelling and note the other.

Parent corrections this build (C727-C733; #559 used C702-C708):

- C727. Doorway reconciliation: the legs disagree on D2 and D6 substance, on D3 letter, and on D7. The parent follows leg 1 on D2 substance (the "inherited the workshop" clause is the claim, and it is undocumented and contradicted -- the father's Utrecht workshop went to Mignon in 1672, and Cornelis worked self-employed in the north from 1667), on D6 substance (same element, load-bearing), on D3 letter ("jewel-like" appears in no source; the series fails letter claims that add an unsourced epithet), and on D7 (three misstatements C710-C712 plus the C719 imprecision, all evidenced from entry boundaries and #557's own pages tables; leg 3's D7 letter HOLD repeats the "5177 #556 Depth line" error and does not address C711 or C712). The parent follows leg 3 nowhere against leg 1; leg 3 agrees with leg 1 on the D2, D4 and D6 letter fails. The check is recorded as a NON-HOLD on the substance and a NON-HOLD on the letter -- the fifty-fifth non-hold; the streak stands at one hundred and thirty-two holds and fifty-five non-holds. Both legs' verdicts are stated in the doorway check above, with the parent's reasons.
- C728. #559's Rejected paragraph says "All 17 remaining #556 carried names (Peeters removed, now the subject of this study)", but the #559 file's own #556 block lists 16 names (1 Reserve + 2 Alternates + 13 Spares) and its header says "(The 16 names carried in #556...". The parent follows the block's own count, verified by name extraction in this run: 16. The "17" in #559's Rejected paragraph is wrong; this study's Rejected paragraph says 16.
- C729. Doorway tally arithmetic (leg 1 C718, carried): 132 holds + 54 non-holds = 186 against 180 checks run before this study -- six more than the checks run. After this check the log's method gives 132 holds / 55 non-holds against 181 checks. The excess is pre-existing in the log, not introduced by this leg or this build. The parent carries the log's own tally (132/55) and records the excess honestly; no reconciliation of the historical count is attempted, because the early checks' per-check records are not re-derivable from the log as it stands.
- C730. Leg 2's nine assembler corrections, all applied in the build: (1) f02, f06 and f13 are three photographs of the SAME painting (Ghent MSK 1907-B, W10) -- count once; (2) f14 and f17 are two photographs of the SAME painting (Salzburg, W11) -- count once; (3) the 1670 date for W10 (Ghent) comes from a single secondary listing and is REPORTED, not verified -- do not present it as a museum date; (4) the 1695 date and 65 x 50 cm size for W20 (Mauritshuis) come from the museum's 1910 abridged catalogue and a secondary survey, not from a Mauritshuis page read end to end -- REPORTED, not verified; (5) f15 (lemon and red crab) and f23 (frogs) were seen ONLY as black-and-white reproductions -- no colour claims for either, and the frogs of f23's title are not clearly identifiable in the reproduction; (6) W1, W3 and W5 are TEXT-ONLY -- their descriptions come from museum or auction-house text, not from images seen by this leg; (7) the early group has two different 1658 anchors that must not be merged: W5 is the Stadel copper (69.8 x 87.1 cm, garden balustrade), W3 is the Sotheby's canvas c. 1657 (64.4 x 78 cm, grotto and landscape) -- different supports, different sizes, different pictures; (8) the strong blues and the small scale are Wikipedia/Grove diagnostics (via the Wikipedia article read end to end), not this leg's invention; (9) the NOMA vanitas reading is the museum's, from its object lesson read end to end, and is attributed to NOMA in M14 -- it is not this leg's symbolism.
- C731. The top-pick paragraph for #561 in this file uses the corrected framing ("the son trained in his father's Antwerp studio, member of the Antwerp Guild in 1660"), not leg 3's "the son who inherited the workshop" wording, which fails on the same ground as D2/D6 (see C709, C743). The Verendael death day-month (11 August) is flagged as carried from #559 and needing a source check (leg 1 C724).
- C732. The #559 study file was left untouched -- its 17-name form is #559's historical record.
- C733. Carried-block adjustments: the #559 new block in this study's Future-candidates section was adjusted from 17 to 16 names (Cornelis de Heem removed, now the subject; Nicolaes van Verendael promoted to top pick), with 8 new names added from leg 3 (2 Alternates, 6 Spares), for 24 names in "New from #560". No other carried block changes: Cornelis appears in no deeper block (the #556 block and all deeper blocks carry verbatim). Leg 2 (works): 8 searches; 5 pages read end to end, 607 lines (Wikimedia Commons category 160; EN Wikipedia Cornelis de Heem 46; NMWA Tokyo 88; NOMA object lesson 43; Fitzwilliam Museum API record PD.24-1966 270); 25 Commons files downloaded sequentially, byte-verified JPEG, with .url.txt sidecars and MANIFEST.txt in ~/workspace/fly-watch/560-deheem/; 22 distinct works seen as images, 3 text-only (W1, W3, W5). Leg 3 (network): 10 searches, about 2,000 lines; 1 page read end to end, 46 lines (EN Wikipedia Cornelis de Heem); the clara-peeters.md Future candidates section lines 286-471 plus lines 270-513 read in full (244 lines); 16 muse-slug 404 checks on the authenticated GitHub API; whole-log greps for 30+ names. Total article lines counted: 1,119 (leg 1: 466; leg 2: 607; leg 3: 46) -- with the overlap stated plainly (EN Wikipedia Cornelis de Heem read end to end by all three legs, 46 lines each; the NMWA Tokyo page read end to end by legs 1 and 2, 88 lines each; the NOMA object lesson read end to end by legs 1 and 2, 43 lines each). Search lines counted: about 3,423 (leg 1: 1,423; leg 3: about 2,000; leg 2 did not count search lines). Mechanisms: 14 (M1-M14, leg 2, with leg 1's 10 technique findings folded in). Works: 20 main (W1-W20) plus Beyond the 20. Dimensions verified: 2 of 20 (W1 NMWA 44.5 x 72.5; W2 Fitzwilliam 53.3 x 73.8), plus the NOMA text-only work (67.3 x 54.6). Honest limits: no work seen in person by any leg; leg 2 images are Commons reproductions, mostly 1000-1920 px, and f15/f23 were seen only in black and white; the Mauritshuis collection page for Fruit Still Life was not read (its 1695 date and 65 x 50 cm size are reported, not verified); the Minneapolis page was not read (no dimensions given); the Stadel collection page was not read; the KMSKA Antwerp still life exists only as a TIFF on Commons and was not fetched; the Nationalmuseum Stockholm flower-and-fruit TIFF was not fetched; Grove Art Online was not read (paywalled); the RKD was never opened; no Dutch-language sources read end to end; no Cornelis-specific technical study was found; no oeuvre total was established; the Rijksmuseum Vanitas Still-Life with Musical Instruments attribution is contested in secondary sources and the Rijksmuseum page was not read.
